commission. madam president, first of all i should like to thank the honourable members of the european parliament, who have been very consistent in following this file. i have a few comments. interestingly, a couple of studies have been carried out recently which compared levels of productivity growth and development in the eu and in the united states. one was conducted under the leadership of professor van ark of groningen university. there, they have studied the industrial and service sectors, trying to spot the areas in which the gaps between the eu and the us have been narrowing and widening. the communications sector is one area in which europe has done better than the united states. the latest issue of the harvard business review came up with the same conclusion. even though only two reference countries were used – france and germany – productivity growth in europe has been about 15% faster than it has in the united states over the last five years. therefore, the liberalisation of telecommunications within our regulatory framework has provided a real boost for innovation and competition. i am also sure that investment into research and development has been very helpful. all this shows that the right pressure for competition and a push for innovation also boosts productivity growth, which is a major benefit for the whole sector. second, on future issues, i agree with mr clegg that in the long term, facilities-based competition encourages investment. the big challenge in applying these regulations will be how to build a bridge from service-based competition to facilities-based competition. the only way to do it consistently is via the opening-up of legacy networks, which have hitherto been financed under monopoly conditions. where the opening-up of networks will require future investment and may involve risks, these risks must be properly assessed. mr herzog made a very interesting comment. it is very clear that we need both ex ante and ex post regulation. ex ante regulation should be used in cases such as universal services, when there is strong public interest, but once markets are fully competitive, then reasons for broad ex ante regulation will essentially diminish. regulation is not the purpose here. the purpose is high-quality services for the citizens at competitive prices. finally, mrs gill, as far as our measures on the implementation of the regulation are concerned, the commission is extremely strict when it exercises its role as guardian of the treaties. the infringement procedure has three phases. the first is the letter of formal notice, which we have sent out. we are waiting for the reply. when we have received it, we will produce a letter giving a reasoned opinion. if we are not happy with the reply we will take the member state to court. we will be extremely cool and objective in applying these principles, so there is no reason for concern in this respect. i also noted the comments of both mrs read and mr clegg on the accession countries. i am ready to have another look at this and will be happy to discuss that issue another time.

- the next item is the report (a5-0383/2003) by mrs kauppi, on behalf of the committee on economic and monetary affairs, on the proposal for a council directive on the introduction of a special taxation system for diesel used as a fuel for commercial purposes and on the approximation of excise duties on petrol and diesel fuel (com (2002) 410 - c5-0409/2002 - 2002/0191 (cns)).
the committee on economic and monetary affairs and industrial policy has adopted a number of amendments to the proposal. president. - the debate is closed. i regret that the committee on economic and monetary affairs and mrs kauppi are so opposed to the commission's proposal. i have, of course, respect for the fact that many people are opposed to the harmonization of taxes on principle. there is something valuable in the fact that the countries themselves decide on the taxes because they finance a significant part of the welfare, which, as we know, differs in the member states. but while it is an important principle to safeguard the right of national taxation authorities to decide, this principle must not be the enemy of the best. the added value of common tax rates is evident in some areas. the diesel tax is one of them. there are a number of inconveniences with different rates of taxation on diesel in the member states. first, different tax rates prevent equal competition between undertakings in the union. in one country it costs more than in another to transport its products. it creates an imbalance in competition between companies. secondly, this difference stimulates what is known as fuel tourism. i've worked in the trucking industry myself, driving a heavy truck. i know you drive hundreds of miles extra to buy cheap diesel. not only that, but extra tanks are built on the trucks to make the trip extra worthwhile. of course, this fuel tourism also contributes to further environmental destruction. we're putting a lot of unnecessary strain on the roads, and the number of accidents is increasing. this is the price we pay for the great disparities in taxation today. thirdly, this situation of large differences in taxation creates scope for social dumping as the difference between different transport costs in different countries is reinforced. overall, the environment, road safety, equal conditions of competition and the conditions of workers are the losers if we maintain a system within the union with large differences in fuel costs.
madam president, i would like to thank you. commissioner, what is it? this is a sensitive subject, as we can see from the many visits that the committee on economic and monetary affairs has made since we first discussed the diesel tax. the debate here tonight is also a sign of that. i would like to thank mr kauppi for his usual diligent and ambitious work. this time - this is the only time i hope - i must say, however, that the majority of the eldr group do not share her conclusions. we can certainly agree that the commission's proposal contains problems, difficult questions and delicate considerations. my conclusion is that it is a pity that the majority in the committee were not able to build on the good points of the proposal and improve it rather than reject it in its entirety, as both the major political groups are now proposing. i therefore share bolkestein's view in that case, and i will explain why. one: the eldr amendments are about tax coordination, not harmonisation. we propose that the taxes can vary, fluctuate within a broad tax band. it allows member states to make their own assessments without taking away the good from the proposal. two, you're not going to die. the commission proposal is about improving the environment. i think some of the arguments sound like the impenitent's impediment. tax competition can be good, i agree, but not if it is harmful to the environment. environmental and energy taxes are the taxes which i believe can be decided at eu level. three, you're not going to the large differences between member states are damaging to the internal market. to claim that there is no diesel tourism, as kauppi does, is actually to run your head in the sand or possibly in the mind and not see the reality that exists. a modified commission proposal is good for the environment, strengthens the internal market and improves competition. neither the ppe-de group nor the pse group should be able to oppose that in this house. let us not fall into touch anxiety or excessive rhetoric every time we consider a proposal that includes the word taxes.

commission. – mr president, network and information security has become increasingly important, with growing use of the internet and other information and communication technologies. in today’s society, much depends on networks and information systems. yet, in order to fully realise the advantages of the information society, people need to be able to trust the system. it has become clear that we have to make a common effort to strengthen network and information security in europe. in 2001 the commission presented a communication on a policy framework and in february 2003 a proposal to establish a european network and information security agency. now, nine months later, we are about to witness a first reading agreement on enisa. let me take this opportunity to thank the rapporteur, mr paasilinna, for his thorough work, as well as the political groups which have helped to reach a satisfactory compromise with the council. it is very positive that there has been such a strong will to negotiate and to reach an agreement. the european parliament has contributed a lot to this process, as it has to the important telecom-related legislation over the past three years. i am happy that parliament has been able to overcome a number of difficult issues and to reach this compromise. the compromise has to be confirmed today and tomorrow by this house, in order for the telecom council on 20 november 2003 to adopt a political agreement which would allow the agency to start at the beginning of 2004. that is what the european council called for last spring. i want to thank parliament for the support it has given the commission. this support has enabled the council to make concessions on the involvement of industry by introducing a permanent stakeholders' group. although not fully in line with what the commission wanted in terms of composition and voting rules for the management board, the compromise proposal has made the management of the agency considerably lighter and given the executive director the main management tasks. the commission has looked carefully at parliament's compromise amendments and can accept them. the present text from parliament has clarified the objectives and tasks of the agency. it will be a learning process to transpose the tasks into a more operational set of actions, but i am happy to see that there is wide agreement on the general lines of what the agency should do. as for the composition of the management board and its voting rules, the commission would have preferred a reduced composition, together with an extension of the simple majority voting rules. we consider that the extended management board with two-thirds majority voting rules is a potential obstacle to the smooth functioning of agencies. in this specific case the commission has, however, decided to accept a deviation from the proposed framework for regulatory agencies of december 2002, although that should not be considered as a precedent for future agencies. this agency will operate in the sensitive field of security and it urgently needs to be put in place now. i hope member states will do their best to participate in the management board in order to make the agency work successfully. this agency is a major step forward for europe or at least for eeurope. member states have shown a willingness to cooperate actively on this issue in order to strengthen the internal market and to help the european citizens, businesses and administrations make full use of the networks and information systems. this will be a signal both to stakeholders in europe and to countries outside the union that we take the issues of network and information security seriously and that member states and the commission, with the support of this house, are ready to act.
i suggest. mr president, the presidency is aware that communications networks are critical and vital infrastructures in modern economies, and as such they must be safeguarded: their defence and security is a matter of public concern. i personally, on behalf of minister gasparri - who is unfortunately in italy today because of the grave loss that has struck our country and is engaged in a last salute to our soldiers who died in iraq - wish to reiterate the commitment that the presidency has wanted and wants to devote to the issue of network and information security. the european institutions have also shown an awareness of the urgency of joint action to enable the agency to be effectively operational by the beginning of 2004, as indicated by the heads of government at the european council in spring 2003. the internet and communication technologies are certainly the innovations that have most characterised our society in recent years and have influenced the way in which the vast majority of human activities are carried out. beyond the obvious advantages, however, these technologies have introduced into our society a number of new problems linked to the unreliability of the instruments used. security needs have changed rapidly over the past two decades, accompanied by the evolution of networks and computers, which are increasingly ubiquitous in private and public life. broadband connections offer users the possibility to connect to the internet permanently; the new wireless applications allow access from virtually anywhere, and the ability to connect almost everything from personal computers to household appliances to the network continues and will continue to develop people's use of the internet. security management has become a difficult and complex task and the user is confronted daily with issues of availability, integrity, authenticity and confidentiality of data and services. the complexity of technology forces numerous components and subjects to interact with each other and makes human behaviour a crucial factor. computers and networks, however, are not only a source of malfunctions but also a target of cyberattacks. today, a cyber attack can put entire nations in serious trouble. to reduce the likelihood of such an event occurring, each country needs to take strong action to improve the security of its ict infrastructure; however, given the transnational dimension of the problem, it is also necessary to define structures in which national experiences can converge and become a common heritage. the experience of these years has taught us that the sharing of experience and information and, more generally, international cooperation are the most effective weapons for creating a security mindset in the information society. the presidency has therefore made every effort during our term to try to deal with this problem. the proposal for a regulation before parliament today is the result of the growing recognition by our institutions of the need for a common european approach to information security. a common position was reached, partly due to the increased level of concern, both in the public and private sectors, about criminal or even potentially harmful phenomena linked to new technologies. the more essential and central communications networks become to the functioning of modern markets, the greater the commitment to ensuring and delivering a safe and secure environment for those operating at all levels. the agency's objectives include the creation of a joint committee in europe on information security issues, which is necessary to ensure the availability and security of the union's networks and information systems. in this context, the presidency stresses the importance of creating a new safety culture, in which this concept is not only seen as a cost for operators but also as a genuine opportunity for market development, including by increasing consumer confidence. the agency should be able to facilitate the application of community measures relating to network and information security. the assistance provided by the agency will contribute to the implementation of community legislation on the subject, thus contributing to the proper functioning of the internal market. it will be able to strengthen the community's and the member states' capacity to react to problems of the security of network and information systems and to the development of the information society in general. the model proposed for the agency is itself innovative. it is based on the awareness that interventions in the area of network security can only be tackled in close cooperation with all the actors in the communications world: with market operators as well as with users of communication services. our institutions have the opportunity to provide a vital service to european citizens and businesses by setting up this agency, thanks to a possible and desirable agreement at first reading, a sign of great responsibility and a deep understanding of the delicacy of the matter. it was a difficult and complex negotiation, during which the positions of the council and parliament gradually came closer together, demonstrating - i repeat - a great sense of responsibility. i hope, therefore, that in a few days' time i shall be able to celebrate the adoption of this regulation. in conclusion, i would like to thank commissioner liikanen, rapporteur paasilinna, mr van velzen, mrs mann and mrs gorsel, and of course all the other members of the committee.
the committee on legal affairs and citizens' rights has also adopted a number of amendments to the proposal for a directive on the protection of individuals with regard to the processing of personal data by the community institutions and bodies. - (sv) mr president, it appears that we will have a european agency for network and information security from 1 january 2004. i'll have a little the rapporteur. - (sv) mr president, commissioner kinnock, mr president of the council, ladies and gentlemen, the world and life do not stop at the external frontiers of the european union. this is the reason why the dialogue between civilizations and cultures must be strengthened. following the entry into force of the new financial regulation, a basic act or a new legal basis is necessary for the actions benefiting from the grants financed from the old chapter a30 of the budget and for the grants awarded under certain lines of part b. in this context, the commission has therefore presented a total of seven proposals establishing the new action programmes. today more than ever, dialogue between cultures and civilizations has a crucial role to play in ensuring peace and stability in the world. this is the contribution i wished to make, as rapporteur, to the report on the establishment of a community action programme for bodies promoting mutual understanding of relations between the european union and certain regions of the non-industrialised world, adopted unanimously by the committee on foreign affairs, human rights, common security and defence policy on 4 november 2003. the centre, institute or network specialised in the analysis of relations between the european union and certain regions contributes to promoting dialogue between cultures and civilizations. this dialogue is fundamental and essential on this earth and in this world which is our common responsibility to manage and protect. the dialogue between cultures and civilizations must promote the universal and generally applicable value of human rights and freedoms and the defence of human dignity. the concept of man on which the european union is founded implies that the value of each person is equal, unique and inviolable. this common basis of values and this conception of man as our own must also be the guarantor of respect for the different cultures and civilizations. the general objective of this programme is to support the activities of these bodies. according to the commission's proposal, the activities should be in accordance with the annual work programme of a centre, institute or network. the activities supported shall contribute to increasing understanding and dialogue between the european union, the regions covered by the ala, meda, tacis and cards regulations and the candidate countries. on this point too, the european parliament wishes to add that these activities must also contribute, and insist, on strengthening the social, cultural and human partnership as well as the meeting places. furthermore, we wish to extend the duration of the programme until 31 december 2008. therefore, the publication of the commission's evaluation report should be deferred to 31 december 2007 and the appropriations planned beyond 2006 should be the subject of an agreement by the budgetary authority on the financial perspectives for the period after 2006. in order to achieve the objective of strengthening mutual knowledge and understanding, the beneficiary bodies must have a thorough knowledge of the cultural, historical, socio-economic and religious characteristics of the regions concerned. i wanted to underline the report. these activities must contribute not only to political cooperation but also to the establishment of a genuine partnership between people. today, the european union is already providing financial and technical assistance to other parts of the world. in the council of ministers, the governments of the member states declared that they attached great importance to these activities and expressed the will to strengthen mutual knowledge between the european union and the partners benefiting from the aid, and to find a legal basis within the union for this purpose. dialogue and assistance shall be supported by the organisations and bodies specialised in relations between the union and the regions concerned. the activities of such bodies include, for example, studies and analyses of union policy, seminars, round tables and publications. there is already a whole series of associations and bodies active in the field of regional cooperation and dialogue. it is therefore important to ensure the coordination of these activities in order to avoid any double employment. the world and life do not stop at the - madam president, the crisis in the stability and growth pact is a clear demonstration of the instability of the eu's monetary union. we can see how an economic crisis is very rapidly transforming into a political crisis, given the way in which monetary union is currently conceived. we can see how, in practice, there are quite different rules for large countries, such as germany and france, and small countries, such as portugal and ireland. this crisis is not only due to the non-respect of the rules of the stability and growth pact. there is a deeper problem at the root of the problem. the difficulties of germany and france are largely due to weak growth and excessive unemployment. the problem is that, in practice, the eu's monetary union and the stability and growth pact do not provide for anything in terms of the revival of growth and the creation of more jobs. that is why this crisis is likely to worsen. we can also clearly see that the various euro area countries are now likely to be united, having adopted different policies. countries with quite different growth and inflation rates also need different interest and exchange rates. it is not a coincidence that the countries of western europe which are not part of the monetary union benefit, on average, from an economic development much more important than that of the countries of the euro area. the euro is one aspect of the problem. it should be noted that the stability and growth pact is no longer functioning and needs to be amended. it must allow for a more expansive economic policy in times of crisis in order to avoid economic crises. a very large majority of swedes voted against the single currency in a referendum in september. the crisis in the stability and growth pact is further evidence that the decision not to participate in the eu's monetary union was a wise one. it is now time to go further and, in the framework of the intergovernmental conference, to confirm the swedish 'non' in a legally binding exemption at least as detailed as the british one.

that it did not vote in favour of the commission's recommendations'. i quote commissioner solbes. a funny way to be neutral, to vote for one side or the other. the presidency's position has been the text of the commission's conclusions. although he is not here today, i think that we should reflect on the position taken by president junker in the eurogroup and then in ecofin, which i do not think is likely to be contrary to the logic of the european union. the debate between 'big countries' and 'small countries' is not valid. what we have discussed is a political dialectic. i do not believe that the commission has a monopoly on policy and i do not believe that rules are the same as sanctions. monopolies are not a good thing in the economic domain, they are not a good thing in the legal domain. the rules can be interpreted in different ways: the council's legal service has interpreted them in one way, the council has interpreted them in one way. voting is also part of the rules. not only that. sanctions are not the whole, but they are a part of the whole. rules are not the same as sanctions. in substance, the council's decision can be appreciated in that the measures it recommends to france and germany are essentially the same as those proposed to the commission by president prodi. the substance is identical. the difference is not about procedure; if the procedure is identical with the sanctions, or if it can be constructed differently, i shall state that the commission's instructions have been substantially complied with. i believe that sanctions are essential, but that they are only effective if they are not applied. sanctions work if they don't work. sanctions work if they produce their own mechanism of deterrence, prevention, adjustment. that is what happened and that is what is absolutely clear. i don't think that the sanctions were our objective; i think our goal was to follow the rules. and so it was. i do not think we would have reached the point of sanctions even for a small country. i would never have voted for sanctions against a small country which, like two large countries, had complied with the commission's demands. sanctions are part of the rules, they're not all the rules. the main part of the rules is the other. and it's important to anticipate the penalties. seeing that sanctions work from the get-go is the key step. the patience was complete. i concluded by saying that the substance of the form is more important, or that both are important, but that so far too much has been said about one aspect of the procedure, as if it were only sanctions, which is not the case. furthermore, the substance was not discussed. the substance is very simple, and it emerged from the debate with political force and intelligence. we are living in a difficult period in europe, in which there is a dramatic symmetry between the intensity of the problems and the capacity for governance. the intensity of the problems: two wars in two years, the breakdown of the world's geopolitical balance, the collapse of financial markets, the unregulated competition produced by china, the effects produced - perhaps beyond forecasts - by the changeover in some countries, local crises and sectoral crises. on the front, limited capacity for governance. the old nation states no longer have the instruments of national governance and we do not yet have european governance in europe. we have no room for budgetary policy because the stability and growth pact exists and is not being challenged. we have no margin for manoeuvre on the policy of interest rates, which are made neutrally in the constitutional logic of the european bank. we cannot make exchange rate policies because they are decided elsewhere. in 2000, the european economy was doing well, the balance sheets were in order, the value of the euro was lower than the dollar. in 2003, the economy is not doing particularly well, the balance sheets are not in order, the euro is highly valued against the dollar. i think isaac newton would have some difficulty understanding the cause and effect relationship between these two phenomena. we need european governance. i believe that the constitutional treaty provides a basis for such a perspective. i believe that the european continent has found in the growth plan - proposed by the presidency, but now a european plan - a reason for a common commitment in the form of a common economic policy. the first since the euro. in 2003, the european continent began a very intense cycle of structural reforms. a coordinated cycle of structural reforms of the labour market and the welfare state in a european spirit and logic. reforms extended from finland to austria, from the netherlands to france, from germany to italy. the continent is regenerating, investing in its future, changing growth expectations through a cycle of structural interventions and fundamental structural reforms. i think these are the things we need to talk about.

in writing. - (sv) we are voting today against the resolution of parliament on the work of the intergovernmental conference. important parts of the resolution go beyond the convention's proposals. a constitutional/consolidated treaty for the union is desirable in itself, but must be based on a clear demarcation between the power of politicians and the areas of competence of the eu. we particularly dislike the fact that the resolution supports what has been called the 'bridge' clause which gives heads of state or government the possibility of waiving the veto of member states without the need for ratification by the parliaments of the member states. we are also against what the resolution says about the reduction in the number of commissioners, which would mean that each country would lose the right to appoint a commissioner. we are also against the idea of parliament having a full co-decision right on the budget of the european union. finally, it is also unacceptable that amendments to the constitution can be made without unanimity and without ratification by the parliaments of the member states, as the resolution proposes. this would undermine the current system whereby member states hold the treaties. we also reiterate our opposition to a permanent president of the council replacing the rotating presidency.

- (sv) mr president, a gigantic job has been done by many people over many years. however, in my view, the compromise which we have presented today is not acceptable. the proposal implies that the system of differentiated voting according to the shares may continue to exist during a transitional period, in the framework of a derogation from the principle. the second stage will be a review, involving a reform of the system of shares a and b and other forms of shareholding. many believe that this will save the nordic system of plural voting. those who claim that the compromise is acceptable, however, have to face the following question of principle: to what extent does the eu have the right to interfere in the way in which the shareholders of private companies choose to organise themselves? i think it is regrettable that so many members of this parliament accept so easily the violations of the rights of shareholders. person névoque the question of the determining principles relating to the respect of the rights of the shareholders. no one other than the shareholders themselves must be concerned with the way in which they organise their shareholding. it is considered to be a fundamental condition of the market economy. if the compromise is approved, in practice, that would mean that the parliament considers the appropriation of the resources as acceptable. if a person has purchased a share to which are attached several voting rights at the market price, what right does the eu or a state have to require that person to declare that the investment for which he has paid has no value? the swedish government argues that this review clause represents a victory in so far as the review is more effective than other types of shareholding than the simple system of plurality voting. this may be true, but the swedish government knows as we all do that when the review takes place in five years' time, it will not be able or willing to touch on the multitude of forms of shareholding that exist in the eu. the discussion is then probably oriented towards the system of actions a and b. the reason is simple. the actions to vote plural constitute a system clear and simple. unlike most other shareholding systems, it is very simple to see how much voting rights a certain type of share has and it is equally easy to see its value on the market. this system of behaviour is a trap, a secret. the main objective of the new rules for takeover bids is to make the eu an open market and to eliminate obstacles to takeovers. however, the proportion of acquisitions is by no means lower in countries where plural voting actions exist than in states where this system is prohibited. in summary, in my view, the proposal submitted to us is not acceptable, in so far as, first, it infringes the right of ownership, second, it does not respect the agreements concluded and, third, it considers the actions to vote plural as an obstacle to acquisitions.
rapporteur. – mr president, i wish to begin by apologising for failing to present a consistent report. this report might be my last in this parliament and, commissioner, i am not proud of the result.
in writing. - (pt) the culture 2000 programme was created to provide a single funding and programming instrument in the field of european cultural cooperation and thus replaces the raphael, ariane and kaleidoscope sectoral programmes. the programme has a budget of approximately eur 167 million and will end on 31 december 2004. the new framework programme for culture is not expected to start until early 2007. the commission therefore envisages extending the current programme for 2005 and 2006 in order to ensure continuity of the actions undertaken. we think this is a positive step and have therefore voted in favour of this proposal. however, we believe that the amount allocated to this single instrument is clearly insufficient in relation to the needs of cultural cooperation. the amount proposed for 2005 and 2006 - about €67 million - is particularly low, given the enlargement. it is essential to assess the impact of the current programme, as the commission itself recognises that some changes are necessary. for example: increased support for the preservation and restoration of european cultural heritage recognised as cultural heritage by unesco; increased participation and support for cultural cooperation initiatives by local associations and institutions; increased support for the acquisition of cultural facilities by local associations.
in writing. - after years of discussions on the harmonization of public procurement law in europe, the only common sense conclusion has finally been reached: for sensitive issues, it is better not to seek harmonization, but rather to leave the freedom to the states to choose the regime that best suits their economic and cultural context. what a waste of time to get to that point! in particular, we support the compromise reached on defence measures in the event of a hostile takeover bid: some states will be able to grant the boards of directors a certain degree of discretion, while others will be left solely to the general meeting of shareholders. we hope that this provisional compromise will be a lasting one, for it foreshadows a more pragmatic and less uniform conception of the european market, which we believe is the right one. we regret, however, that this compromise did not include the question of golden shares, or 'specific shares' in french terminology, used by some states to safeguard the public interest in large privatised companies, particularly in the face of the risk of foreign takeover. the commission and the court of justice are fighting this system fiercely, but it is protective and needs to be given more legitimacy.
in writing. - i voted against this directive following the rejection of three crucial amendments which aimed at the full and timely information and consultation of workers, both of the bidding company and of the target company, in takeover bids. some argue that this is already guaranteed by other directives and that there is no need to deal with this issue in this directive. this view is shared by commissioner bolkestein. i am not convinced of this, because it would be difficult to understand why the introduction of a minimum dose of social dialogue in this directive would meet with such opposition. but i entirely agree with mr bolkestein that, as it has been voted, with the agreement of the commission and the council, this directive is not a social directive, it is even a clearly anti-social directive! after more than 15 years of debate, i regret to note, as the esc did, that neither the council, the commission nor the majority of parliament have been able to grasp the simple fact that workers are as important as shareholders and that they have a right to be informed and heard in merger operations. this is a sad day for the european social model.
- (sv) mr president, madam commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, we have reached a broad consensus on the budget for the coming years. we owe this success essentially to the fact that we can rely on the rapporteurs, the president of the committee on budgets and a team spirit in this committee which, in my view, should inspire the member states even more, if one judges by what happened in brussels last weekend. i thank the rapporteur and my colleagues for their report. as rapporteur for the previous year, that is to say the 2003 budget, i had this year the main task of implementing the budget and i would like to reflect somewhat on this point. the adoption of the budget is the first, relatively simple, step in the process; it is apparent that the choices are really complicated by the actual implementation. i must admit that i am concerned about this, both for 2003 and for the future, particularly 2004. for several years, we have recorded large surpluses and a high level of liquidity commitments (ral), especially in the context of the structural funds, which involve considerable sums. unfortunately, this is also the situation we are observing today. we recently received an implementation report for 2003 which identifies specific problems, even if some areas are in the process of improvement. we have recently spoken about the phenomenon of the global transfer of the still more important amounts not spent at the end of the year, which must be moved from the budgetary sections where there is a lack of execution vis-à-vis other sections. the situation is even more alarming in category iv, where the lack of resources is very acute. community initiatives for which we face difficulties with a large proportion of resources are also of concern. if the implementation of the information programmes is lagging behind, while we must take on the essential information tasks, notably on the new constitution, enlargement, etc., we are obviously faced with a real problem. the new member states should be seriously concerned about this before 2004, lest they be disappointed when they find that the level of implementation is quite different from that proposed in the budget. for several years, we have adopted a whole series of reports on implementation, etc. in my view, it would be useful for parliament to adopt a more comprehensive strategy to tackle annual surpluses, accumulation of commitments for liquidity and weaknesses in implementation. it could, for example, produce a special report setting out a strategy to deal with the problems at the root of this delay. in the last year, the group of european socialists has put forward a number of proposals aimed at simplification. i think we should reinforce this element, by including a more radical strategic review of the actual implementation of the budget. i have repeatedly observed an absurd situation in my eyes, which leads for example to regions, universities and non-governmental organisations no longer requesting the credits, due to the complexity of the application procedure, on the one hand, and on the other, because it takes so long to receive the payments that the applicants are faced with financial problems - even if their projects have been approved. i think that the solution lies, for example, in the implementation of continuous reform, more modern budgetary techniques, a more up-to-date audit system and a culture of greater openness. in the long term, i think we could even put a question mark on the credit and payment system. we must define financial perspectives that are not compatible with the whole of the sectors for seven years, which will make it impossible to manage. perhaps parliament should also realise that it is impossible to increase the budget without ensuring that the commission has the means indispensable to implement the decisions. the eventual elected members, who will have to enter the house to discuss agenda 2007, will have to face a major challenge from the beginning of the next legislature.
mr president, i would like to thank you. the eu should be an organisation for peace and not an arms dealer. that is why the eu should not sell weapons at all, especially to countries that risk using them in war. with that logic, of course, we should not sell any weapons to the united states in the first place, but neither should we sell any to china. my refusal to export arms to china is not directed solely against china, but is the expression of a general principle. all of this stuff that's been said about china is true, but there are also positive things. for example, a dialogue has been initiated with representatives of the dalai lama, whom the dalai lama himself regards with great hope. of course, such a small step is not enough to change the general picture. it would therefore be wrong to lift the arms embargo on china, but do not forget that there are other countries to which we should not sell arms.
in writing. - (sv) we would like to emphasise the fact that it is important that we finally have a statute which means the end of the system of indirect remuneration, which has existed in parliament until now. we are in favour of a system which allows reimbursement of actual travel costs. a system that provides for a single salary - even if it is higher than the current salary of many members - is more appropriate, because it is an open system, which the electorate can examine and evaluate. despite this basic approach, we would like to make it clear that we consider the level of pay recommended in parliament's june 2003 report to be too high and that we will continue to press our government to persuade the council to adopt a more reasonable level of pay.
in writing. - (de) it is extremely urgent to have common rules which are open, clear and unequivocal. we therefore decided to vote in favour of the resolution, but we do not support the proposals calling for a lowering of the retirement age. we also believe that salaries should be closer to those of members of the swedish parliament and that national taxes should apply to salaries.
in writing. - (sv) the european parliament has today voted on a proposal for a directive to harmonise the rules applicable to environmental liability within the european union. the principle of 'polluter pays' must be the paramount one. the idea is to make businesses and other actors across the union subject to identical legislation as regards their obligations to prevent, mitigate and pay for the environmental damage for which they are responsible. i have taken a more environmentally friendly line than that advocated by the group of the european people's party (christian democrats) and the european democrats and have therefore voted for stricter standards on nuclear pollution (amendment no 42) and on gmos (genetically modified organisms) (amendments nos 43 and 44). although amendments nos 42 and 44 received a majority of the votes, the required 314 votes were not obtained and these amendments were therefore rejected.
in writing. - (sv) i voted in favour of amendments nos 20 and 23, which were rejected. i voted for these amendments because there is a clear risk that products which are currently considered as food supplements will be redefined as medicines in the future. these products will then become less accessible while many people supplement their diet with food supplements in order to feel better and healthier. if food supplements were to be redefined as medicines, the directive could have a considerable impact on prices. a price increase for private consumers could make food supplements too expensive. this could result in an increase in disease and an increase in costs to society.
mr president, i would like to thank you. thank you for your reply, commissioner. as the ngos say, there is a strong concern about trafficking in women and children in the border area between germany and the czech republic, but also in other parts of europe. this is a major and common problem which is also present in the border areas of the other countries which border the present and enlarged eu but which are not members of the eu. i live in northern sweden and we have the problem in the barents region. unlike the commissioner, i do not believe that the media reports are increasing trade, but i believe that it is poverty and the subordination of women. this promotes trafficking in women and children. we have to work very broadly. i also believe that legal prostitution promotes trafficking in women and children. that's why i want sex buyers to be criminalised. i think it's a good way. i hear that the commission and the commissioner are taking this matter very seriously, and i think that is important. i would also like to emphasize the daphne programme. does the commissioner think that the police in the eu and the candidate countries are cooperating sufficiently well to deal with this major problem? is there progress in police cooperation?
mr president, mr commissioner, i would like to thank you for your report. this is not a question of whether or not genetically modified organisms are. i am one of those who believe that neither the whole of mankind nor the european part of mankind can escape this knowledge and technology. it is, however, urgent that we apply the precautionary principle, that we apply the polluter pays principle and that we respect people's anxieties about the new. so we must be able to mark and give a proper message. we have had and still have differing views on the matter, but i would like to congratulate graefe zu baringdorf on this report. we have been able to agree on a position of the european parliament on all the essential points, which i hope will send a signal to the commission and to the council. i think it's very well balanced, but i have a few comments. i am disappointed to hear the commissioner so vehemently opposing paragraph 3 on common rules. the winds actually blow across national borders. it is not reasonable to have one rule on one side of a national border and another rule on the other. this is about life and the content of life. if we are to achieve anything with this, we must have common rules. i would ask you to consider this in the commission before you make any hasty decisions. however, i can agree with the commission a little bit on paragraph 9. i think it would be dangerous to continue with a moratorium if we don't get what we want. i think that paragraph 9 is correct, but i think that it is perhaps going a little too far. i am not prepared to vote for that item. that is the only point in the report on which i have a different opinion. the commissioner said that the eu had to be more flexible in its approach to the problem of the environment. i had in my foolishness imagined that democratic decisions are always voluntary. then there can be no trouble. i think it is the right of a region or a country to say 'we don't want this'. we can't force people to use a technology if they don't want to, even though i think the technology is good. it is very important that we get this signal and that we can be relatively united tomorrow when we take decisions. we have different views on whether or not genetically modified organisms are or are not, but we want to be careful. we now want to come up with a proposal on how we can arrange coexistence so that we are credible to all the people who will live on the food produced, either one way or the other.
in writing. - (sv) i voted in favour of the european parliament's draft general budget for the financial year 2004. although it includes commitments totalling €200 million for the rehabilitation and reconstruction of iraq for the period 2003-04, on the whole the budget is quite tight - it ultimately represents only around 1% of the cumulative gdp of the member states foreseen for 2004. it is also important that parliament keeps up the pressure on the commission to continuously improve the control and accounting of the eu budget. measures should be taken against fraud and swindling. it is urgent and important to link agriculture and the environment more clearly. the reform of the common agricultural policy must include investments to improve food quality and to combat animal diseases, a view shared by parliament. the christian democrats also believe that subsidies for tobacco cultivation should be phased out, as should export refunds, which distort world market prices and disadvantage developing countries. it is just unfortunate that such proposals have not received the support of parliament. as vice-president of the committee on development and cooperation, my colleague mr wijkman has done a good job of ensuring that the eu's development policy is primarily aimed at achieving the so-called millennium development goals, particularly in the fight against poverty. parliament has adopted this view, as have the wording in the aid budget to make development policy more results-oriented.
in writing. - (sv) i should like to express my satisfaction at the rejection of amendment no 5 by the group of the party of european socialists (by 229 votes against, 209 votes for and 22 abstentions). it criticised the italian presidency for not contributing to the adoption of the sixth research framework programme with regard to embryonic stem cell research. in member states such as germany, austria, portugal and ireland, it is illegal, and in the case of ireland even unconstitutional, to carry out research on human embryos. it is therefore particularly positive, and in keeping with the healthy spirit of the european idea, that a majority in the european parliament shows that it respects the legislation of these countries and does not oblige their taxpayers to finance illegal activities. the italian presidency's contribution to the council on this point cannot therefore be criticised, as the pse group has tried to do. rather, her efforts should be considered commendable and worthy of respect.
in writing. - (sv) as moderates, we are opposed to the european parliament deciding how to treat serious diseases in the member states. health care policy is not a matter for the eu. we invite the members of the committee on petitions to focus on issues falling within the eu's competence. the principle of subsidiarity must be fully respected. ultimately, the patient and the family must have a considerable influence on the choice of health care and health professionals. the doctor has the final responsibility for the care and treatment, but he must advise the patient and suggest the various possible alternatives. the rights and influence of the patient can also be strengthened if the patient has the possibility to change doctors if the relationship between them is not satisfactory or if there is no trust. this way of guaranteeing the patient the best possible care and treatment is the opposite of the european parliament's interference in personal healthcare matters.
the commission is not prepared to accept this. as a christian democrat and swedish member of the european parliament, i am very pleased that the question of moldova has been raised again. moldova has not succeeded in stabilising its democratic development since the collapse of the soviet union. as commissioner busquin knows very well, the question of language and cultural identity is a divisive one for the entire moldovan people, i.e. the question of the position of the romanian language and the moldovan national movement in society versus the position of the russian language and the russian and eastern orientation. we european and swedish christian democrats wish to express our support for the opposition in moldova, led by the christian democratic party under iurie rosca. we call on them to be bold in their efforts to promote a european, democratic and pluralist orientation of moldovan society, based on the common values on which the european union is ultimately founded. what this resolution says about the fact that moldovan democracy has not been stabilized is a very correct statement. the situation in transnistria, which is in effect a breakaway republic, does not make the situation any easier. i believe that the commission and the european council should act to strengthen the cultural development of moldova, so that the country can achieve independence and its own language, moldovan, can have its rightful place. the mass demonstrations in chisinau in recent years have shown that the country must be given full support for a democratic, pluralist development and that the communist leadership in the country must not stifle the opposition.
